5. See Franco Sciannameo, “Giacinto Scelsi c’est moi!” in Outrage: Art, Controversy, and Society, ed. Richard Howells, Andreea Deciu Ritivoi, and Judith Schachter, 262–77 (London: Palgrave Macmillan, 2012).
6. La Spezia, It.: Luna Editore, 1993. 2nd ed. 2001.
7. Michelle Biget, “Giacinto Scelsi: Un approccio al pianoforte,” in Castanet and Cisternino, eds., Giacinto Scelsi: Viaggio al centro del suono, 198–227.
8. See Giacinto Scelsi: Im Innem des Tons: Symposionsberichte des Musikfestes Hamburg (Hofheim, Ger.: Wolke Verlag, 1992); and see also Gianmario Borio, “Il suono come processo: Scelsi e i compositori di Nuova Consonanza,” in Catalogo del 42º Festival di Nuova Consonanza, 8–19, Rome, October 27–December 17, 2005.
9. In pp. 191–201 of Daniela Tortora, ed., Giacinto Scelsi nel centenario della nascita: Atti dei Convegni Internazionali (Rome: Aracne, 2008).
10. In Tortora, ed., Giacinto Scelsi nel centenario della nascita, 119–44.
11. Thorsten Wagner, Franco Evangelisti und die Improvisationsgruppe Nuova Consonanza, (Saarbrücken: Pfau, 2004). Mario Bertoncini, Arpe Eolie and Other Useless Things (Milan: Die Schachtel, 2007). Gianmario Borio, “Zur Vorgeschichte der Klangkomposition,” in Klangperspektiven, ed. Lukas Haselböck, 27–43 (Hofheim, Ger.: Wolke, 2011).
12. In Rivista Italiana di Musicologia 34, no. 2 (1999): 347–87.
13. An earlier version of chapter 5 was translated by Christine Anderson from the Italian and published in the May 2004 edition of the German journal MusikTexte as “Wer ist Walt(h)er Klein? Ein Beitrag zur Biographie Giacinto Scelsis” (no. 101, pp. 37–43). According to Martinis, that original article solicited further research on the subject, and in fact some unpublished material was later brought to light by various scholars, therefore enabling Martinis to mount an exhibit of Scelsi and Klein memorabilia at a special Kontinent Scelsi section of the 2007 Salzburg Festival. But back in August 2003, when Martinis originally wrote this essay, only one Italian source referenced Walther Klein, consisting of a short biographical entry in Bibliografia essenziale (updated to July 1974 by Sergio Martinotti). This was later published as a second appendix in Luigi Rognoni’s volume La scuola musicale di Vienna (Torino: Einaudi, 1974), itself a republication of Espressionismo e Dodecafonia (Torino: Einaudi, 1954). Page 545 of this republication offered a spare reference to Klein, pointing toward his article “Das theosophische Element in Schönbergs Weltanschauung,” which was part of a special Arnold Schönberg edition of Musikblätter des Anbruch (7, nos. 8–9 [1924]).
Martinis’s essay published in MusikTexte—“Wer ist Walt(h)er Klein?”—prompted a reply from Professor Stefan Drees, who mentioned, among other things, an important cache of documents concerning the Klein/Scelsi relationship, preserved at the University of California at Berkeley’s music library. For details, see Horst Weber and Manuela Schwartz, ed., Quellen zur Geschichte emigrierter Musiker, 1933–1950: Sources Relating to the History of Emigré Musicians, 1933–1950 (Munich: K. G. Saur Verlag, 2003): 50–51.
For her part, musicologist Christine Anderson contacted the Schönberg Foundation in Vienna, where she discovered three letters Walther Klein had written to Schönberg, discussing theosophy. Anderson also discovered that Klein had contributed a series of articles to the journal Musikblätter des Anbruch that could prove useful in identifying further cultural ties between Klein and Scelsi. A list of those articles follows.
“Kosmischmusikalische Entsprechungslehre,” Musikblätter des Anbruch 14 (1920): 483–86.
“Kosmischmusikalische Entsprechunglehre,” part 2, 15 (1920): 531–34.
“Aus den Prager Tagen,” Musikblätter des Anbruch 15 (1920): 518–2.
“Polnisches Symphonie-Konzert: Emil Miynarski,” Musikblätter des Anbruch 6 (1921): 121–22.
“Das Harmoniesystem der neueren Musik. aus der Einleitung zur ‘Harmonielehre für Vorgeschrittene,’” Musikblätter des Anbruch 1–2 (1922): 9–10.
“Das theosophische Element in Schönbergs Weltanschauung,” Musikblätter des Anbruch 7–8 (1924): 272–74.
“Von der Magie des Tanzes: Tanz in dieser Zeit,” Musikblätter des Anbruch 1926 (3–4): 100–1.
Furthermore, research into Klein’s compositions brought to light some interesting works such as Invocation (Anrufung): Komposition für Geige und Klavier mit Worten von Annie Besant (Innsbruck: Universitäts–Verlag Wagner, 1924). It had been performed in July of the year prior at the Conference of the European Federation of the Theosophical Society in Vienna.
14. The Scelsi/Klein correspondence was first examined by Stefan Drees in “Walt(h)er Klein und Giacinto Scelsi: Zur Feststellung einiger Tatsachen” MusikText 102 (August 2004): 80. Friedrich Jaecker took up the discussion a few months later in “Der Dilettant und die Profis” MusikText 104 (Februar 2005): 27–40; and again in 2008 with “Giacinto Scelsi e Walther Klein” in Tortora, ed., Giacinto Scelsi nel centenario della nascita, 191–201; and in 2009 with “Il dilettante e i professionisti: Giacinto Scelsi, Vieri Tosatti, e Co.” Musica/Realtà 88 (March): 93–122. On February 18, 2010, Franco Sciannameo delivered the lecture “Alla ricerca dell’atonalità espressiva: Lettere inedite di Scelsi a Klein,” at the Incontri al Museo Casa Scelsi in Rome.
